\widehat{E}_{i}=\mathsf{CC}_{i}\cup\bigcup_{C\in{\cal C}_{i}}E(C)
. So, if
\{u,v\}\in\mathsf{CC}_{i}
, then
v\in\widehat{\mathsf{N}}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i})}(u)
; otherwise, there exists
C\in{\cal C}_{i}
such that
\{u,v\}\in E(C)
. Therefore, there exists
v^{\prime}\in V(G)
such that
\{\{u,v\},\{u,v^{\prime}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
, so
\{v,v^{\prime}\}\in\mathsf{NeiPairs}_{{\cal C}_{i}}(u)
, thus
v\in\bigcup\mathsf{NeiSubsets}(u)
. So, for every
u\in u^{*}\in\mathsf{EQ}
,
\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(u)=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets}(u))
is a vertex type.
∎

5.3
Robot Type

Now, we continue to introduce the variables we need for the ILP instance. We have a variable for each
robot type
. First, we show how we derive a robot type for each robot from a solution, and then we will present the definition of an abstract type. An intuition for a robot type is as follows. In Definition
37
, where we derive vertex types, we saw how we derive
\mathsf{NeiSubsets}(u)
for each
u\in V(G)\setminus\mathsf{VC}
. Some of the multisets in
\mathsf{NeiSubsets}(u)
are derived from
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i})
, where
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
is an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair associated with each robot
i
. Now, we look on the “puzzle” from the perspective of the robots. For each
u\in V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i}))\setminus\mathsf{VC}
, the multiset
\widehat{\mathsf{N}}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i})}(u)
is “allocated” for the vertex type of
u
.
For an induced submultigraph
\overline{H}
of
\overline{G}
, we present two notations:
the multiset
\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\overline{H})
, and an
allocation
for this multiset. When
\overline{H}=\overline{G}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i}))
,
\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\overline{H})
is the set of pairs
(u^{*}_{i},\mathsf{NeiSub})
we will later allocate to vertex types.

Definition 40

(
\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\overline{H})
).

Let
G
be a connected graph and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Let
\overline{H}
be a submultigraph of
\overline{G}
. Then,
\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\overline{H})=\{(u^{*}_{i},\widehat{\mathsf{N}}_{\overline{H}}(u^{*}_{i}))~|~u^{*}_{i}\in V(\overline{H})\}
as a multiset.

A
vertex allocation
of
\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\overline{H})
is a function that assigns a vertex type to each
(u^{*}_{i},\mathsf{NeiSub})\in\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\overline{H})
. We allocate
(u^{*}_{i},\mathsf{NeiSub})
to a vertex type that “expects” to get
\mathsf{NeiSub}
, that is, a vertex type
(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})
where
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
.

Definition 41

(
Vertex Allocation of
\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\overline{H})
).

Let
G
be a connected graph and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Let
\overline{H}
be a submultigraph of
\overline{G}
. A vertex allocation of
\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\overline{H})
is a function
\mathsf{Alloc}_{\overline{H}}:\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\overline{H})\rightarrow\mathsf{VerTypeS}
such that for every
(u^{*}_{i},\mathsf{NeiSub})\in\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\overline{H})
,
\mathsf{Alloc}_{\overline{H}}(u^{*}_{i},\mathsf{NeiSub})=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})
where

\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
.

In addition to the vertex allocation, the type of each robot
i
is associated with a vector of non-negative integers
\mathsf{NumOfCyc}=(N_{i,2},N_{i,3},N_{i,5},N_{i,6},\ldots,N_{i,2|\mathsf{VC}|})
: for every
2\leq j\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|
,
j\neq 4
,
N_{i,j}
is the number of cycles of length exactly
j
in
{\cal C}_{i}
, where
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
be an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair.

Definition 42

(
Deriving Robot Types From a Solution).

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
and let
\widehat{E}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{E}_{k}
such that the conditions of Lemma
15
hold. For every
1\leq i\leq k
, let
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
be an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair. For every
1\leq i\leq k
, let
G^{\prime}_{i}=\overline{G}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i}))
with an isomorphism
\alpha_{i}:V(G_{i}^{\prime})\rightarrow V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i}))
, and let
\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}_{i}=E(G_{i}^{\prime})
. For every
1\leq i\leq k
and
(u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub})\in\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(G^{\prime}_{i})
, let
\mathsf{Alloc}_{G_{i}^{\prime}}((u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub}))=\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(\alpha_{i}(u^{*}_{j}))
. For every
1\leq i\leq k
and
2\leq j\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|
,
j\neq 4
, let
N_{i,j}
be the number of cycles of size
j
in
{\cal C}_{i}
, and for every
1\leq i\leq k
let
\mathsf{NumOfCyc}_{i}=(N_{i,2},N_{i,3},N_{i,5},N_{i,6},\ldots,N_{i,2|\mathsf{VC}|})
. Then, for every
1\leq i\leq k
, let
\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(\{(\mathsf{CC}_{j},{\cal C}_{j})\}_{1\leq j\leq k},i)=(\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}_{i},\mathsf{Alloc}_{G_{i}^{\prime}},\mathsf{NumOfCyc}_{i})
.

Whenever
\{(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
is clear from context, we refer to
\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(\{(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})\}_{1\leq i\leq k},

i)
as
\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(i)
.

Now, we define the term robot type. As mentioned, a robot type is first associated with
\mathsf{CC}\subseteq E(\overline{G})
and a vertex allocation of
\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}))
. We demand that
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})
is connected, every vertex in
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})
has even degree and
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}))
, similarly to Conditions
2
and
3
of Definition
34
. This way we ensure that the multiset
\widehat{E}
we will build for the a robot makes
\mathsf{Graph}(\widehat{E})
connected: we will later associate with a robot only cycles
C
such that
V(C)\cap V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}))\neq\emptyset
. In addition, we also ensure that every vertex in
\mathsf{Graph}(\widehat{E})
will have even degree in
\mathsf{Graph}(\widehat{E})
: each such vertex has even degree in
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})
, and we will add only cycles to this graph, so the degree of each vertex will remain even. Therefore, we ensure that the “local” properties of each robot, given by Conditions
1
and
2
of Lemma
15
, are preserved. The vector of non-negative integers
\mathsf{NumOfCyc}=(N_{2},N_{3},N_{5},N_{6},\ldots,N_{2|\mathsf{VC}|})
determines how many cycles of each length (other than
4
) we will associate with the robot. Observe that, due to Condition
4
of Definition
34
, each
N_{j}
is bounded by
2|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}
.

Definition 43

(
Robot Type).

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Then,
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})
is a robot type if the following conditions are satisfied:

1.

\mathsf{CC}\subseteq E(\overline{G})
.

2.

\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})
is connected, every vertex in
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})
has even degree and

v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}))
.

3.

\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})}
is a vertex allocation of
\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}))
.

4.

\mathsf{NumOfCyc}=(N_{2},N_{3},N_{5},N_{6},\ldots,N_{2|\mathsf{VC}|})
, where
0\leq N_{i}\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}
for every
2\leq i\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|
,
i\neq 4
.

We denote the set of robot types by
\mathsf{RobTypS}
.

In the following lemma we show the “correctness” of Definition
42
, that is, for every
1\leq i\leq k
,
\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(i)
is indeed a robot type.

Lemma 44
.

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Let
\widehat{E}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{E}_{k}
such that conditions of Lemma
15
hold, and for every
1\leq i\leq k
, let
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
be an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair. Then, for every
1\leq i\leq k
,
\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(i)
is a robot type.

Proof.

Let
1\leq i\leq k
. We show that
\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(i)
is a robot type by proving that the conditions of Definition
43
hold.
Since
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
is an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair, from Condition
1
of Definition
34
,
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i})
is a
\overline{G}
-submultigraph. So,
G^{\prime}_{i}=\overline{G}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i}))
is a submultigraph of
\overline{G}
, with an isomorphism
\alpha_{i}:V(\overline{G})\rightarrow V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i}))
satisfies the conditions of Definition
29
. Thus,
\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}_{i}=E(G_{i}^{\prime})\subseteq E(\overline{G})
, and therefore, Condition
1
of Definition
43
holds.

From Condition
2
of Definition
34
,
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i})
is connected,
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i}))
and every vertex in
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i})
has even degree. So,
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}_{i})
is connected, every vertex in
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}_{i})
has even degree and
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}_{i}))
. Thus, Condition
2
of Definition
43
holds.

Now, let
(u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub})\in\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(G^{\prime}_{i})
. Then,
\mathsf{Alloc}_{G_{i}^{\prime}}((u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub}))=\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(\alpha_{i}(u^{*}_{j}))
, so
\mathsf{Alloc}_{G_{i}^{\prime}}((u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub}))=(z^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets}(\alpha_{i}(u^{*}_{j})))
, where
\mathsf{NeiSubsets}(\alpha_{i}(u^{*}_{j}))=\bigcup_{1\leq t\leq k}

(\{\widehat{\mathsf{N}}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{t})}(\alpha_{i}(u^{*}_{j}))\}\cup\mathsf{NeiPairs}_{{\cal C}_{t}}(\alpha_{i}(u^{*}_{j})))
(see Definition
37
). Observe that
\widehat{\mathsf{N}}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i})}

(\alpha_{i}(u^{*}_{j}))=\mathsf{NeiSub}
, thus
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}(\alpha_{i}(u^{*}_{j}))
. In addition, from Condition
2
of Definition
29
,
\alpha_{i}(u^{*}_{j})\in u^{*}
, so
z^{*}=u^{*}
. Also, from Lemma
39
,
\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(\alpha_{i}(u^{*}_{j}))\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
. Therefore,
\mathsf{Alloc}_{G_{i}^{\prime}}
is a vertex allocation of
\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(G_{i}^{\prime})=\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i}^{\prime}))
, so Condition
3
of Definition
43
holds.

Now, since
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
is an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair, from Condition
1
of Definition
34
, at most
2|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}
cycles in
{\cal C}_{i}
have length other than
4
. Therefore, for every
2\leq j\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|
,
0\leq N_{j}\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}
. So, Condition
4
of Definition
43
holds.

We proved that all of the conditions of Definition
43
hold, so
\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(i)
is a robot type. This ends the proof.
∎

5.4
Cycle Type

Lastly, we have a variable for each
cycle type
. For every
1\leq i\leq k
, let
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
be an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair. First, we show how we derive a cycle type for each
C\in{\cal C}_{i}
, for every
1\leq i\leq k
, and then we will present the definition.
An intuition for a cycle type is as follows. In Lemma
37
, where we derive vertex types, we saw how we derive
\mathsf{NeiSubsets}(u)
for each
u\in V(G)\setminus\mathsf{VC}
. Some of the multisets in
\mathsf{NeiSubsets}(u)
are derived from cycles in
\mathsf{CC}_{i}
, for some
1\leq i\leq k
. Now, we look on the “puzzle” from the perspective of the cycles. For each
\{\{u,v\},\{u,v^{\prime}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
, the multiset
\{v,v^{\prime}\}
is “allocated” for the vertex type of
u
.

Similarly to the definition of a vertex allocation of
\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(G^{\prime})
(Definition
41
), we have the following definition, for allocation of
\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
:

Definition 45

(
Vertex Allocation of
\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
).

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
and let
C\in\mathsf{Cyc}_{G}\cup\mathsf{Cyc}_{G^{*}}
. A vertex allocation of
\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
is a function
\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C}:\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)\rightarrow\mathsf{VerType}
, such that for every
\{\{v_{i-1},v_{i}\},\{v_{i},v_{i+1}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
,
\{v_{i-1},v_{i+1}\}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
and
v_{i}\in u^{*}
or
v_{i}=u^{*}
, where
\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C}(\{\{v_{i-1},v_{i}\},

\{v_{i},v_{i+1}\}\})=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})
.

Let
C=(v_{0},\ldots,v_{\ell}=v_{0})\in\mathsf{Cyc}_{G}
. Recall that we denote by
\mathsf{EQ}(C)
the cycle in
G^{*}
obtained from
C
by replacing each
v_{i}\in V(C)\cap(V(G)\setminus\mathsf{VC})
by
u^{*}\in\mathsf{EQ}
, where
v_{i}\in u^{*}
, for every
1\leq i\leq\ell
. Similarly, let
\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C}
be a vertex allocation of
\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
. We denote by
\mathsf{EQ}(\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C})
the function
\mathsf{EQ}(\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C}):\mathsf{EdgePairs}(\mathsf{EQ}(C))\rightarrow\mathsf{VerType}
defined as follows: for every
\{\{v_{i-1},u^{*}\},\{u^{*},v_{i+1}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(\mathsf{EQ}(C))
,
\mathsf{EQ}(\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C})(\{\{v_{i-1},u^{*}\},\{u^{*},v_{i+1}\}

\})=\mathsf{Alloc}_{C}(\{\{v_{i-1},v_{i}\},\{v_{i},v_{i+1}\}\})
. Observe that
\mathsf{EQ}(\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C})
is a vertex allocation of
\mathsf{EdgePairs}(\mathsf{EQ}(C))
:

Observation 46
.

Let
C\in\mathsf{Cyc}_{G}
and let
\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C}
be a vertex allocation of
\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
. Then,
\mathsf{EQ}(\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C})
is a vertex allocation of
\mathsf{EdgePairs}(\mathsf{EQ}(C))
.

Definition 47

(
Deriving Cycle Types From a Solution).

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
and let
\widehat{E}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{E}_{k}
such that conditions of Lemma
15
hold. For every
1\leq i\leq k
, let
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
be an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair. For every
1\leq i\leq k
,
C\in{\cal C}_{i}
and
\{\{u,v\},\{u,v^{\prime}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
, let
\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{i,C}(\{\{v,u\},\{u,v^{\prime}\}\})=\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(u)
. Then, for every
1\leq i\leq k
and
C\in{\cal C}_{i}
, let

\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(\{(\mathsf{CC}_{j},{\cal C}_{j})\}_{1\leq j\leq k},i,C)=(\mathsf{EQ}(C),\mathsf{EQ}(\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{i,C}),\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(i))
.

Whenever
\{(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
is clear from context, we refer to
\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(\{(\mathsf{CC}_{j},{\cal C}_{j})\}_{1\leq j\leq k},

i,C)
as
\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(i,C)
.

Now, we define the term cycle type. In addition to the vertex allocation of
\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
, we have the robot type
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})
associated with the cycle type. In order to maintain the connectivity of
\mathsf{Graph}(\widehat{E})
, we demand that
V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}))\cap V(C)\cap\mathsf{VC}\neq\emptyset
(see the discussion before Definition
43
).

Definition 48

(
Cycle Type).

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Let
C\in\mathsf{Cyc}_{G^{*}}
, let
\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C}
be a vertex allocation of
\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
and let
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})
be a robot type. Then,
\mathsf{CycTyp}=(C,\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C},\mathsf{RobTyp})
is a cycle type if
V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}))\cap V(C)\cap\mathsf{VC}\neq\emptyset
.

We denote the set of cycle types by
\mathsf{CycTypS}
.

In the following lemma we show the “correctness” of Definition
47
, that is, for every
1\leq i\leq k
,
C\in{\cal C}_{i}
,
\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(i,C)
is indeed a cycle type.

Lemma 49
.

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Let
\widehat{E}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{E}_{k}
such that conditions of Lemma
15
hold and for every
1\leq i\leq k
let
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
be an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair. Then, for every
1\leq i\leq k
,
C\in{\cal C}_{i}
,
\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(i,C)
is a cycle type.

Proof.

Let
1\leq i\leq k
and
C\in{\cal C}_{i}
. We show that
\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(i,C)
is a cycle type. First, from Condition
4
of Definition
34
, every cycle in
{\cal C}_{i}
of length other than
4
is simple. So, the length of
C
is at most
\mathsf{max}\{4,2|\mathsf{VC}|\}
, and thus,
\mathsf{EQ}(C)\in\mathsf{Cyc}_{G^{*}}
.

Second, we show that
\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{i,C}
, defined in Definition
47
, is a vertex allocation of
\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
. Let
\{\{u,v\},\{u,v^{\prime}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
. Then,
\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{i,C}(\{\{v,u\},\{u,v^{\prime}\}\})=\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(u)
. First, from Lemma
39
,
\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(u)
is a vertex type. Now, by Definition
37
,
\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(u)=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets}(u))
, where
u\in u^{*}
,
\mathsf{NeiSubsets}(u)=\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq k}(

\{\widehat{\mathsf{N}}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{j})}(u)\}\cup\mathsf{NeiPairs}_{{\cal C}_{j}}(u))
and
\mathsf{NeiPairs}_{{\cal C}_{j}}(u)=\{\{v,v^{\prime}\}~|~C\in{\cal C}_{j},\{\{u,v\},\{u,v^{\prime}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)\}
. So, since
\{\{u,v\},\{u,v^{\prime}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
, then
\{v,v^{\prime}\}\in\mathsf{NeiPairs}_{{\cal C}_{i}}(u)\subseteq\mathsf{NeiSubsets}(u)
. Therefore,
\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{i,C}
is a vertex allocation of
\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
, and from Observation
46
,
\mathsf{EQ}(\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{i,C})
is a vertex allocation of
\mathsf{EdgePairs}(\mathsf{EQ}(C))
.

Now, since
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
is an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair, from Condition
3
of Definition
34
,
V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i}))

\cap\mathsf{VC}=V(\mathsf{Graph}(\widehat{E}_{i}))\cap\mathsf{VC}
. From Definition
42
,
\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(i)=((\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}_{i}),\mathsf{Alloc}_{G_{i}^{\prime}},

\mathsf{NumOfCyc}_{i})
, where
\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}_{i}=E(G_{i}^{\prime})
and
G^{\prime}_{i}=\overline{G}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i}))
. Now, Condition
1
of Definition
29
implies that
V(G^{\prime}_{i})\cap\mathsf{VC}=V(\mathsf{Graph}(\widehat{E}_{i}))\cap\mathsf{VC}
, so
V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}))\cap\mathsf{VC}=V(\mathsf{Graph}(\widehat{E}_{i}))

\cap\mathsf{VC}
. From Observation
33
,
V(\mathsf{Graph}(\widehat{E}_{i}))\cap\mathsf{VC}\cap V(C)\neq\emptyset
, thus
V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}))\cap\mathsf{VC}\cap V(C)\neq\emptyset
. In addition, from Lemma
44
,
\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(i)
is a robot type. Overall,
\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(i,C)
is a cycle type.
∎

5.5
The Instance
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
of the ILP Problem

Now, we are ready to present our reduction to the ILP problem. We have the following variables:

•

For every
\mathsf{VerTyp}\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
, we have the variable
x_{\mathsf{VerTyp}}
.

•

For every
\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
, we have the variable
x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}
.

•

For every
\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}
, we have the variable
x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}
.

Each variable stands for the number of elements of each type.
We give intuition for each of the following equations:

Equation
1
: Robot Type for Each Robot.
In this equation, we make sure that the total sum of robot types is exactly
k
, that is, there is exactly one robot type for each robot:

1.
\displaystyle{\sum_{\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}}x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}=k}
.

Equation
2
: Vertex Type for Each Vertex.
For every
u^{*}\in\mathsf{EQ}
, we denote the set of
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
by
\mathsf{VerTypS}_{u^{*}}
. In this equation, we make sure, for every
u^{*}\in\mathsf{EQ}
, that the total sum of vertex types in
\mathsf{VerTypS}_{u^{*}}
is exactly
|u^{*}|
. That is, there is exactly one vertex type
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})
for each
u\in u^{*}
:

2. For every
u^{*}\in\mathsf{EQ}
,
\displaystyle{\sum_{\mathsf{VerTyp}\in\mathsf{VerTypS}_{u^{*}}}x_{\mathsf{VerTyp}}=|u^{*}|}

Equation
3
: Assigning Enough Subsets to Each Vertex Type.
We have the following notations:

•

For every
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
, every
\mathsf{NeiSub}=\{v,v^{\prime}\}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
and
1\leq j\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|
, we denote

\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},j)=\{\mathsf{CycTyp}=(C,\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C},\mathsf{RobTyp})\in\mathsf{CycTypS}~|~

|\{\{\{u^{*},v\},\{u^{*},v^{\prime}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)~|~

\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C}(\{\{u^{*},v\},\{u^{*},v^{\prime}\}\})=\mathsf{VerTyp}\}|=j\}
. That is,
\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},j)
is the set of cycle types that assign
\mathsf{NeiSub}
to
\mathsf{VerTyp}
exactly
j
times.

•

For every
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
, every
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
and
1\leq j\leq 2^{|\mathsf{VC}|}+|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}
,

\mathsf{RobTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},j)=\{\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})\in\mathsf{RobTypS}~|~

|\{(u^{*}_{i},\mathsf{NeiSub})\in\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}))~|~\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})}((u^{*}_{i},\mathsf{NeiSub}))=\mathsf{VerTyp}\}|=j\}
. That is,
\mathsf{RobTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},j)
is the set of robot types that assign
\mathsf{NeiSub}
to
\mathsf{VerTyp}
exactly
j
times.

Recall that, for a vertex
u\in u^{*}\in\mathsf{EQ}
of vertex type
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})
,
\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
encodes “how” the edges incident to
u
are covered. That is, for every
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
, there exists a robot
i\in[k]
which covers the multiset of edges with one endpoint in
u
and the other being a vertex in
\mathsf{NeiSub}
. A robot is able to cover this exact multiset of edges if
(u^{*}_{i},\mathsf{NeiSub})\in\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i})
or
\mathsf{NeiSub}=\{v,v^{\prime}\}
and
\{\{u^{*},v\},\{u^{*},v^{\prime}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
for
C\in{\cal C}_{i}
, where
\mathsf{CC}_{i}
and
{\cal C}_{i}
satisfy the conditions of Lemma
35
. Therefore, for every
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
and every
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
, we ensure we have at least one
\mathsf{NeiSub}
assigned to each vertex of
\mathsf{VerTyp}
:

3. For every
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
, and every
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
,

\displaystyle{\sum_{j=1}^{2{|\mathsf{VC}|}}\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},j)}}j\cdot x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}+}

\displaystyle{\sum_{j=1}^{2^{|\mathsf{VC}|}+|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}}\sum_{{\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},j)}}j\cdot x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}\geq x_{\mathsf{VerTyp}}}
.

Observe that Equations
1
–
3
ensure that every edge with one endpoint in
V\setminus\mathsf{VC}
is covered.

Equation
4
: Covering Each Edge with Both Endpoints in
\mathsf{VC}
.
We have the following notations:

•

For every
\{u,v\}\in E
such that
u,v\in\mathsf{VC}
,

\mathsf{CycTypS}(\{u,v\})=\{\mathsf{CycTyp}=(C,\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C},\mathsf{RobTyp})\in\mathsf{CycTypS}~|~\{u,v\}\in E(C)\}
. That is,
\mathsf{CycTypS}(\{u,v\})
is the set of cycle types
\mathsf{CycTyp}=(C,\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C},\mathsf{RobTyp})
where
C
covers
\{u,v\}
.

•

For every
\{u,v\}\in E
such that
u,v\in\mathsf{VC}
,

\mathsf{RobTypS}(\{u,v\})=\{\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},

\mathsf{NumOfCyc})\in\mathsf{RobTypS}~|~\{u,v\}\in\mathsf{CC}\}
. That is,
\mathsf{RobTypS}(\{u,v\})
is the set of robot types
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})})
where
\mathsf{CC}
covers
\{u,v\}
.

In this equation we ensure that each
\{u,v\}\in E
with both endpoints in
\mathsf{VC}
is covered at least once:

4. For every
\{u,v\}\in E
such that
u,v\in\mathsf{VC}
,

\displaystyle{\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\{u,v\})}}x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}+\sum_{{\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}(\{u,v\})}}x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}\geq 1}
.

Observe that Equations
1
–
4
ensure that every edge in
G
is covered.

Equation
5
: Assigning the Exact Amount of Cycles with Length Other Than
4
to Each Robot Type.
We have the following notation:

•

For every
\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
and for every
2\leq j\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|
,

\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{RobTyp},j)=\{\mathsf{CycTyp}=(C,\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C},\mathsf{RobTyp})\in\mathsf{CycTypS}~|~|C|=j\}
. That is,
\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{RobTyp},j)
is the set of cycle types, stand for a cycle of length
j
and assigned to a robot with robot type
\mathsf{RobTyp}
.

Let
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
be a robot type. In this equation, we verify that the number of cycles of length other than
4
assigned to robots with robot type
\mathsf{RobTyp}
is exactly as determined in
\mathsf{NumOfCyc}
:

5. For every
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
and for every

2\leq j\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|
,
j\neq 4
,
\displaystyle{\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{RobTyp},j)}}x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}=N_{j}\cdot x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}}
,

where
\mathsf{NumOfCyc}=(N_{2},N_{3},N_{5},N_{6},\ldots,N_{2|\mathsf{VC}|})
.

Equation
6
: Verifying the Budget Limit.

Let
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})

\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
be a robot type, and let
i\in[k]
be robot with robot type
\mathsf{RobTyp}
. From Lemma
35
, there exist
\mathsf{CC}_{i}\subseteq\widehat{E}_{i}
and a multiset of cycles
{\cal C}_{i}
in
\mathsf{Graph}(\widehat{E}_{i})
, such that
\mathsf{CC}_{i}\cup\bigcup_{C\in{\cal C}_{i}}E(C)=\widehat{E}_{i}
. Now, as we determine the robot type of the
i
-th robot to be
\mathsf{RobTyp}
,
|\mathsf{CC}_{i}|=|\mathsf{CC}|
, and the number of cycles in
{\cal C}_{i}
of length other than
4
is fixed by
\mathsf{NumOfCyc}
. We have the following notation:

•

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Bud}(\mathsf{RobTyp})=|\mathsf{CC}|+\sum_{2\leq j\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|,j\neq 4}N_{j}\cdot j}
, where
\mathsf{NumOfCyc}=(N_{2},N_{3},N_{5},N_{6},\ldots,

N_{2|\mathsf{VC}|})
.

That is,
\mathsf{Bud}(\mathsf{RobTyp})
is the number of edges in
\mathsf{CC}_{i}\cup\bigcup_{C\in{\cal C}_{i}}E(C)
excluding the number of edges in cycles of length
4
in
{\cal C}_{i}
.
Therefore,
B-\mathsf{Bud}(\mathsf{RobTyp})
is the budget left for the robot for the cycles in
{\cal C}_{i}
of length
4
. Now, we take the largest number which is a multiple of
4
and less or equal to
B-\mathsf{Bud}(\mathsf{RobTyp})
, to be the budget left for cycles of length
4
. So, we have the following notation:

•

For every
\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
,
\mathsf{CycBud}(\mathsf{RobTyp})=\lfloor(B-\mathsf{Bud}(\mathsf{RobTyp}))\cdot\frac{1}{4}\rfloor\cdot 4
.

6. For every
\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
,

\displaystyle{\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{RobTyp},4)}}4\cdot x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}\leq x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}\cdot\mathsf{CycBud}(\mathsf{RobTyp})}
.

Summary of Equations.
In conclusion, given an instance
(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
of
CGE
problem,
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
is the instance of the ILP problem described by the following equations:

1.

\displaystyle{\sum_{\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}}x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}=k}
.

2.

For every
u^{*}\in\mathsf{EQ}
,
\displaystyle{\sum_{\mathsf{VerTyp}\in\mathsf{VerTypS}_{u^{*}}}x_{\mathsf{VerTyp}}=|u^{*}|}
.

3.

For every
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
, and every
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
,

\displaystyle{\sum_{j=1}^{2{|\mathsf{VC}|}}\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},j)}}j\cdot x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}+}

\displaystyle{\sum_{j=1}^{2^{|\mathsf{VC}|}+|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}}\sum_{{\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},j)}}j\cdot x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}\geq x_{\mathsf{VerTyp}}}
.

4.

For every
\{u,v\}\in E
such that
u,v\in\mathsf{VC}
,

\displaystyle{\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\{u,v\})}}x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}+\sum_{{\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}(\{u,v\})}}x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}\geq 1}
.

5.

For every
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
and for every

2\leq j\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|
,
j\neq 4
,
\displaystyle{\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{RobTyp},j)}}x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}=N_{j}\cdot x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}}
,

where
\mathsf{NumOfCyc}=(N_{2},N_{3},N_{5},N_{6},\ldots,N_{2|\mathsf{VC}|})
.

6.

For every
\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
,

\displaystyle{\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{RobTyp},4)}}4\cdot x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}\leq x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}\cdot\mathsf{CycBud}(\mathsf{RobTyp})}
.

5.6
Correctness: Forward Direction

Now, we turn to prove the correctness of the reduction. In particular, we have the following lemma:

Lemma 50
.

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
and let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
. Then,
(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
is a yes-instance of the
CGE
, if and only if
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
is a yes-instance of the
Integer Linear Programming
.

We split the proof of the correctness of Lemma
50
to two lemmas. We begin with the proof of the first direction:

Lemma 51
.

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
and let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
. If
(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
is a yes-instance of the
CGE
problem, then
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
is a yes-instance of the
Integer Linear Programming
.

Towards the proof of Lemma
51
, we present the function
\mathsf{RobExpToILP}
. This function gets as input, for every
1\leq i\leq k
,
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
that is an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair. Then, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
,
\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(z)
is the number of elements of type
z
derived by Definitions
37
,
42
and
47
:

Definition 52

(
\mathsf{RobExpToILP}
).

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
and let
\widehat{E}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{E}_{k}
such that conditions of Lemma
15
hold. For every
1\leq i\leq k
, let
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
be an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair. Then:

1.

For every
\mathsf{VerTyp}\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
,
\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(\{(\mathsf{CC}_{j},{\cal C}_{j})\}_{1\leq j\leq k},\mathsf{VerTyp})=|\{u\in V(G)\setminus\mathsf{VC}~|~\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(u)=\mathsf{VerTyp}\}|
.

2.

For every
\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
,
\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(\{(\mathsf{CC}_{j},{\cal C}_{j})\}_{1\leq j\leq k},\mathsf{RobTyp})=|\{1\leq i\leq k~|~\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(i)=\mathsf{RobTyp}\}|
.

3.

For every
\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}
,
\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(\{(\mathsf{CC}_{j},{\cal C}_{j})\}_{1\leq j\leq k},\mathsf{CycTyp})=|\{(i,C)~|~1\leq i\leq k,C\in{\cal C}_{i},\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(i,C)=\mathsf{CycTyp}\}|
.

Whenever
\{(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
is clear from context, we refer to

\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(\{(\mathsf{CC}_{j},{\cal C}_{j})\}_{1\leq j\leq k},\mathsf{VerTyp})
,
\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(\{(\mathsf{CC}_{j},{\cal C}_{j})\}_{1\leq j\leq k},\mathsf{RobTyp})
and

\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(\{(\mathsf{CC}_{j},{\cal C}_{j})\}_{1\leq j\leq k},\mathsf{CycTyp})
as
\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(\mathsf{VerTyp})
,
\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(\mathsf{RobTyp})

and
\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(\mathsf{CycTyp})
, respectively.

In the next lemma, we prove that the values given by Definition
52
satisfy the inequalities of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
:

Lemma 53
.

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
and let
\widehat{E}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{E}_{k}
such that conditions of Lemma
15
hold. For every
1\leq i\leq k
, let
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
be an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair. Then, the values
x_{z}=\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(z)
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, satisfy the inequalities of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
.

For the sake of readability, we split Lemma
53
and its proof into two lemmas: In Lemma
54
we prove that the values
x_{z}=\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(z)
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, satisfy inequalities
1
–
3
of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
, and in Lemma
55
we prove that these values satisfy inequalities
4
–
6
.

Lemma 54
.

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
and let
\widehat{E}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{E}_{k}
such that conditions of Lemma
15
hold. For every
1\leq i\leq k
, let
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
be an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair. Then, the values
x_{z}=\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(z)
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, satisfy inequalities
1
–
3
of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
44
, for every
1\leq i\leq k
,
\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(i)
is a robot type, and thus, Equation
1
is satisfied.

Similarly, from Lemma
39
, for every
u\in u^{*}\in\mathsf{EQ}
,
\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(u)
is a vertex type, where
\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(u)=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})
, for some
\mathsf{NeiSubsets}\subseteq 2^{\mathsf{N}_{G^{*}}(u^{*})\times\mathsf{2}}
. Therefore, Equations
2
are satisfied.

Now, let
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
, let
u\in u^{*}
such that
\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(u)=\mathsf{VerTyp}
, and let
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
. So, by Definition
37
,
\mathsf{NeiSubsets}=\mathsf{NeiSubsets}(u)=\bigcup_{1\leq i\leq k}(\{\widehat{\mathsf{N}}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i})}(u)\}\cup\mathsf{NeiPairs}_{{\cal C}_{i}}(u))
, where
\mathsf{NeiPairs}_{{\cal C}_{i}}(u)=\{\{v,v^{\prime}\}~|~C\in{\cal C}_{i},\{\{u,v\},

\{u,v^{\prime}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)\}
. Thus, there exists
1\leq i\leq k
such that at least one among the following conditions holds:

1.

\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiPairs}_{{\cal C}_{i}}(u)
. Therefore, there exists
C\in{\cal C}_{i}
such that
\{\{u,v\},\{u,v^{\prime}\}\}\in

\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
and
\mathsf{NeiSub}=\{v,v^{\prime}\}
. Thus, by Definition
47
,
\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(i,C)=(\mathsf{EQ}(C),\mathsf{EQ}(\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{i,C})
,
\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(i))
, where
\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{i,C}
is a vertex allocation of

\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
, and
\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{i,C}(\{\{v,u\},\{u,v^{\prime}\}\})=\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(u)=\mathsf{VerTyp}
. So,
u
contributes at least one to
|\{\{\{u,v\},\{u,v^{\prime}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)~|~

\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{i,C}(\{\{u,v\},\{u,v^{\prime}\}\})=\mathsf{VerTyp}\}|
, thus,
u
contributes at least one to
|\{\{\{u^{*},v\},\{u^{*},v^{\prime}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(\mathsf{EQ}(C))~|~

\mathsf{EQ}(\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{i,C})(\{\{u^{*},v\},\{u^{*},v^{\prime}\}\})=\mathsf{VerTyp}\}|
. Now, since
C\in\mathsf{Cyc}_{G}
,
\mathsf{EQ}(C)\in\mathsf{Cyc}_{G^{*}}
, so the length of
\mathsf{EQ}(C)
is bounded by
\mathsf{max}\{4,2|\mathsf{VC}|\}
, and thus,
|\mathsf{EdgePairs}(\mathsf{EQ}(C))|\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|
. In addition, by Lemma
49
,
\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(i,C)=\mathsf{CycTyp}
is a cycle type. Therefore, there exists
1\leq t\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|
such that
\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},t)
. Thus,
u
contributes at least one to
\displaystyle{\sum_{r=1}^{2{|\mathsf{VC}|}}\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},r)}}r\cdot x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}}
.

2.

\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\widehat{\mathsf{N}}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i})}(u)
. By Definition
42
,
\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(i)=(\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}_{i},\mathsf{Alloc}_{G_{i}^{\prime}},\mathsf{NumOfCyc}_{i})
, where (i)
\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}_{i}=E(G_{i}^{\prime})
, (ii)
G^{\prime}_{i}=\overline{G}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i}))
with an isomorphism
\alpha_{i}:V(G_{i}^{\prime})\rightarrow V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i}))
and (iii) for every
(u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub})\in\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(G^{\prime}_{i})
,
\mathsf{Alloc}_{G_{i}^{\prime}}((u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub}))=\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(\alpha_{i}(u^{*}_{j}))
. Let
j\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\alpha_{i}(u^{*}_{j})=u
, so
\mathsf{Alloc}_{G_{i}^{\prime}}((u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub}))=\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(\alpha_{i}(u^{*}_{j}))=\mathsf{VerTyp}
. Therefore,
u
contributes at least one to
|\{(u^{*}_{t},\mathsf{NeiSub})\in\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i}))~|~

\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i})}((u^{*}_{t},\mathsf{NeiSub}))=\mathsf{VerTyp}\}|
. In addition, by Lemma
44
,
\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(i)=\mathsf{RobTyp}
is a robot type. Now, since
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i})
is a
\overline{G}
-submultigraph (see Definition
27
),
|V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i}))\cap u^{*}|\leq 2^{|\mathsf{N}_{G^{*}}({u^{*}})|}+|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}\leq 2^{|\mathsf{VC}|}+|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}
, so
|\{(u^{*}_{t},\mathsf{NeiSub})\in\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i}))|\leq 2^{|\mathsf{VC}|}+|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}
. Therefore, there exists
1\leq t\leq 2^{|\mathsf{VC}|}+|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}
such that
\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},t)
. Thus,
u
contributes at least one to
\displaystyle{\sum_{r=1}^{2^{|\mathsf{VC}|}+|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}}\sum_{{\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},r)}}r\cdot x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}}
.

Therefore, each
u\in V(G)\setminus\mathsf{VC}
such that
\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(u)=\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})
, contributes at least one to

\displaystyle{\sum_{j=1}^{2{|\mathsf{VC}|}}\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},j)}}j\cdot x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}+\sum_{j=1}^{2^{|\mathsf{VC}|}+|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}}}

\displaystyle{\sum_{{\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},j)}}j\cdot x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}}
.

Thus, since
\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(\mathsf{VerTyp})=|\{u\in V(G)\setminus\mathsf{VC}~|~\mathsf{DerVerTyp}(u)=\mathsf{VerTyp}\}|
and
x_{\mathsf{VerTyp}}=\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(\mathsf{VerTyp})
, we get that Equations
3
are satisfied.
∎

Lemma 55
.

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
and let
\widehat{E}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{E}_{k}
such that conditions of Lemma
15
hold. For every
1\leq i\leq k
, let
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
be an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair. Then, the values
x_{z}=\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(z)
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, satisfy inequalities
4
–
6
of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
.

Proof.

Let
\{u,v\}\in E
such that
u,v\in\mathsf{VC}
. By Condition
3
of Lemma
15
,
E(G)\subseteq\widehat{E}_{1}\cup\ldots\cup\widehat{E}_{k}
. So, there exists
1\leq i\leq k
such that
\{u,v\}\in\widehat{E}_{i}
. Since
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
is an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair, by Condition
6
of Definition
34
,
\mathsf{CC}_{i}\cup\bigcup_{C\in{\cal C}_{i}}E(C)=\widehat{E}_{i}
. So, at least one among the following two cases holds:

1.

\{u,v\}\in\mathsf{CC}_{i}
. By Definition
42
,
\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(i)=(\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}_{i},\mathsf{Alloc}_{G_{i}^{\prime}},\mathsf{NumOfCyc}_{i})
, where
\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}_{i}=E(G_{i}^{\prime})
and
G^{\prime}_{i}=\overline{G}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i}))
. So,
\{u,v\}\in\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}_{i}
. Now, by Lemma
44
,
\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(i)=\mathsf{RobTyp}
is a robot type. Therefore,
\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}(\{u,v\})
, thus
\displaystyle{\sum_{{\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}(\{u,v\})}}x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}\geq 1}
.

2.

There exists
C\in{\cal C}_{i}
such that
\{u,v\}\in E(C)
. By Definition
47
,
\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(i,C)=(\mathsf{EQ}(C),\mathsf{EQ}(\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{i,C})
, and observe that
\{u,v\}\in\mathsf{EQ}(C)
. In addition, by Lemma
49
,
\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(i,C)=\mathsf{CycTyp}
is a cycle type. Therefore,
\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\{u,v\})
, so
\displaystyle{\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\{u,v\})}}x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}\geq 1}
.

Either way, we get that
\displaystyle{\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\{u,v\})}}x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}+\sum_{{\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}(\{u,v\})}}}

x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}\geq 1
, so Equations
4
are satisfied.

Now, let
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
, let
2\leq j\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|
,
j\neq 4
, and let
1\leq i\leq k
such that
\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(i)=\mathsf{RobTyp}
. By Definition
42
,
N_{j}
is the number of cycles of length
j
in
{\cal C}_{i}
. Now, for every
C\in{\cal C}_{i}
, by Definition
47
,
\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(i,C)=(\mathsf{EQ}(C),\mathsf{EQ}(\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{i,C})
, and in particular,
|C|=|\mathsf{EQ}(C)|
. In addition, by Lemma
49
, for every
C\in{\cal C}_{i}
,
\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(i,C)=\mathsf{CycTyp}
is a cycle type. Therefore,
|\{C\in{\cal C}_{i}~|~\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(i,C)=(\mathsf{EQ}(C),\mathsf{EQ}(\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{i,C}),|\mathsf{EQ}(C)|=j\}|=N_{j}
. So, Equations
5
are satisfied.

Now, let
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
, and let
1\leq i\leq k
such that
\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(i)=\mathsf{RobTyp}
. Since
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
is an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair, by Condition
6
of Definition
34
,
\mathsf{CC}_{i}\cup\bigcup_{C\in{\cal C}_{i}}E(C)=\widehat{E}_{i}
. So,
|\widehat{E}_{i}|=|\mathsf{CC}_{i}|+\sum_{C\in{\cal C}_{i}}|C|
. By Definition
42
,
\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(i)=(\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}_{i},\mathsf{Alloc}_{G_{i}^{\prime}},\mathsf{NumOfCyc}_{i})
, where
\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}_{i}=E(G_{i}^{\prime})
and
G^{\prime}_{i}=\overline{G}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}_{i}))
. Thus,
|\mathsf{CC}|=|\mathsf{CC}^{\prime}_{i}|=|\mathsf{CC}_{i}|
. In addition, by Definition
42
, for every
2\leq j\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|
,
j\neq 4
,
N_{i,j}
is the number of cycles of size
j
in
{\cal C}_{i}
, where
\mathsf{NumOfCyc}=(N_{2},N_{3},N_{5},N_{6},\ldots,N_{2|\mathsf{VC}|})
. Moreover, by Condition
5
of Definition
34
, the cycles in
{\cal C}_{i}
of length other than
4
are simple. So, for every
C\in{\cal C}_{i}
,
|C|=4
or
2\leq|C|\leq{2|\mathsf{VC}|}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle{|\widehat{E}_{i}|=|\mathsf{CC}_{i}|+\sum_{C\in{\cal C}_{i}}|C|=|\mathsf{CC}|+\sum_{2\leq j\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|,j\neq 4}\sum_{C\in{\cal C}_{i},|C|=j}j+\sum_{C\in{\cal C}_{i},|C|=4}4=}

\displaystyle{|\mathsf{CC}|+\sum_{2\leq j\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|,j\neq 4}N_{j}+\sum_{C\in{\cal C}_{i},|C|=4}4}
.

Recall that
\displaystyle{\mathsf{Bud}(\mathsf{RobTyp})=|\mathsf{CC}|+\sum_{2\leq j\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|,j\neq 4}N_{j}}
. Thus,

\displaystyle{|\widehat{E}_{i}|=\mathsf{Bud}(\mathsf{RobTyp})+\sum_{C\in{\cal C}_{i},|C|=4}4=\mathsf{Bud}(\mathsf{RobTyp})+|\{C\in{\cal C}_{i}~|~|C|=4\}|\cdot 4}
. Now, by Condition
4
of Lemma
15
,
|\widehat{E}_{i}|\leq B
. So,
\mathsf{Bud}(\mathsf{RobTyp})+|\{C\in{\cal C}_{i}~|~|C|=4\}|\cdot 4\leq B
, implies that
|\{C\in{\cal C}_{i}~|~|C|=4\}|\leq(B-\mathsf{Bud}(\mathsf{RobTyp}))\cdot\frac{1}{4}
. Observe that
|\{C\in{\cal C}_{i}~|~|C|=4\}|\in\mathbb{N}
, thus
|\{C\in{\cal C}_{i}~|~|C|=4\}|\leq\lfloor(B-\mathsf{Bud}(\mathsf{RobTyp}))\cdot\frac{1}{4}\rfloor
, so
|\{C\in{\cal C}_{i}~|~|C|=4\}|\cdot 4\leq\lfloor(B-\mathsf{Bud}(\mathsf{RobTyp}))\cdot\frac{1}{4}\rfloor\cdot 4=\mathsf{CycBud}(\mathsf{RobTyp})
. Therefore, the number of cycles of length
4
in
{\cal C}_{i}
is bounded by
\mathsf{CycBud}(\mathsf{RobTyp})\cdot\frac{1}{4}
.

Now, for every
C\in{\cal C}_{i}
, by Definition
47
,
\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(i,C)=(\mathsf{EQ}(C),\mathsf{EQ}(\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{i,C})
,
\mathsf{DerRobTyp}(i))=(\mathsf{EQ}(C),\mathsf{EQ}(\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{i,C})
,
\mathsf{RobTyp})
. In addition, by Lemma
49
,
\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(

i,C)
is a cycle type. So, for every
C\in{\cal C}_{i}
,
\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(i,C)\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{RobTyp},4)
if and only if
|C|=4
. Therefore,
|\{C\in{\cal C}_{i}~|~\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(i,C)\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{RobTyp},4)\}|=|\{C\in{\cal C}_{i}~|~|C|=4\}|\leq\mathsf{CycBud}(\mathsf{RobTyp})\cdot\frac{1}{4}
. Now, for every
\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}
,
x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}=\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(\mathsf{CycTyp})=|\{(i,C)~|~1\leq i\leq k,C\in{\cal C}_{i},\mathsf{DerCycTyp}(i,C)=\mathsf{CycTyp}\}|
. Thus,
i
contributes at most
\mathsf{CycBud}(\mathsf{RobTyp})\cdot\frac{1}{4}
to
\displaystyle{\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{RobTyp},4)}}x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}}
. So,

\displaystyle{\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{RobTyp},4)}}4\cdot x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}\leq x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}\cdot\mathsf{CycBud}(\mathsf{RobTyp})}
.
Therefore, Equations
6
are satisfied. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemmas
54
and
55
conclude the correctness of Lemma
53
.

Now, we invoke Lemma
53
in order to prove the correctness of Lemma
51
:

Proof.

Assume that
(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
is a yes-instance of the
CGE
problem. By Lemma
15
, there exist
k
multisets
\widehat{E}^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{E}^{\prime}_{k}
such that the conditions of Lemma
15
hold. So, by Observation
24
, there exist
\widehat{E}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{E}_{k}
such that the conditions of Lemma
15
hold and for every
1\leq i\leq k
, each
\{u,v\}\in\widehat{E}_{i}
appears at most twice in
\widehat{E}_{i}
. Thus, for every
1\leq i\leq k
, by Lemma
35
, there exists
(\mathsf{CC}_{i},{\cal C}_{i})
that is an
\widehat{E}_{i}
-valid pair. By Lemma
53
, the values
x_{z}=\mathsf{RobExpToILP}(z)
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, satisfy the inequalities of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
. Therefore,
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
is a yes-instance of the
Integer Linear Programming
.
∎

5.7
Correctness: Reverse Direction

In the next lemma, we state the revers direction of Lemma
51
:

Lemma 56
.

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
and let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
. If
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
is a yes-instance of
Integer Linear Programming
, then
(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},

k,B)
is a yes-instance of the
CGE
problem.

Towards the proof of Lemma
56
, given values
x_{z}
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, that satisfy the inequalities of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
, we show how to construct multisets
\widehat{E}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{E}_{k}
satisfy the conditions of Lemma
15
. Recall that intuitively, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
,
x_{z}
stands for the number of elements of type
z
in a solution. First, we define a vertex type for each vertex in
V(G)\setminus\mathsf{VC}
. To this end, for every
u\in u^{*}\in\mathsf{EQ}
, we arbitrary picks a vertex type
(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
such that the total number of vertices we chose for a vertex type
\mathsf{VerTyp}
is exactly
x_{\mathsf{VerTyp}}
:

Definition 57

(
Deriving Vertex Types from an ILP Solution).

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Let
x_{z}
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, be values that satisfy the inequalities of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
. For every
u^{*}\in\mathsf{EQ}
, let
\mathsf{VerTypS}_{u^{*}}=\{(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\}
and
\mathsf{VerTypFun}_{u^{*}}:u^{*}\rightarrow\mathsf{VerTypS}_{u^{*}}
be such that for every
\mathsf{VerTyp}\in\mathsf{VerTypS}_{u^{*}}
,
|\{u\in u^{*}~|~\mathsf{VerTypS}_{u^{*}}(u)=\mathsf{VerTyp}\}|=x_{\mathsf{VerTyp}}
. Then, for every
u\in u^{*}\in\mathsf{EQ}
,
\mathsf{ILPDerVerTyp}(\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\},u)=\mathsf{VerTypS}_{u^{*}}(u)
.

Whenever
\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\}
is clear from context, we refer to
\mathsf{ILPDerVerTyp}(\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\},u)
as
\mathsf{ILPDerVerTyp}(u)
.

Observe that since
x_{z}
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, satisfy the inequalities of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
, then, in particular, Equations
2
are satisfied. That is, for every
u^{*}\in\mathsf{EQ}
,
\sum_{\mathsf{VerTyp}\in\mathsf{VerTypS}_{u^{*}}}x_{\mathsf{VerTyp}}=|u^{*}|
. Therefore, for every
u^{*}\in\mathsf{EQ}
, there exists a function
\mathsf{VerTypS}_{u^{*}}
such that for every
\mathsf{VerTyp}\in\mathsf{VerTypS}_{u^{*}}

|\{u\in u^{*}~|~\mathsf{VerTypS}_{u^{*}}(u)=\mathsf{VerTyp}\}|=x_{\mathsf{VerTyp}}
, as defined in Definition
57
. Thus,
\mathsf{ILPDerVerTyp}
is well defined.

Next, we define a robot type for each robot. For every
i\in[k]
, we arbitrary determine a robot type for the
i
-th robot such that the total number of robots we choose for a robot type
\mathsf{RobTyp}
is exactly
x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}
:

Definition 58

(
Deriving Robot Types from an ILP Solution).

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Let
x_{z}
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, be values that satisfy the inequalities of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
. Let
\mathsf{RobTypFun}:[k]\rightarrow\mathsf{RobTyps}
be such that for every
\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
,
|\{i\in[k]~|~\mathsf{RobTypFun}(i)=\mathsf{RobTyp}\}|=x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}
. Then, for every
1\leq i\leq k
,
\mathsf{ILPDerRobTyp}(\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\},i)=\mathsf{RobTypFun}(i)
.

Whenever
\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\}
is clear from context, we refer to
\mathsf{ILPDerRobTyp}(\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\},i)
as
\mathsf{ILPDerRobTyp}(i)
.

Observe that since
x_{z}
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, satisfy Equation
1
, so
\sum_{\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}}x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}=k
. Thus, there exists a function
\mathsf{RobTypFun}:[k]\rightarrow\mathsf{RobTyps}
such that for every
\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
,
|\{i\in[k]~|~\mathsf{RobTypFun}(i)=\mathsf{RobTyp}\}|=x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}
, as defined in Definition
58
. Therefore,
\mathsf{ILPDerRobTyp}
is well defined.

Let
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
. Recall that in order to cover the edges adjacent to a vertex
u
of type
\mathsf{VerTyp}
, we need to “allocate” each
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
to
u
. An allocation of
\mathsf{NeiSub}
can be derived from a robot of a robot type
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
, if
(u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub})\in\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})
and

\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})}(u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub})=\mathsf{VerTyp}
. In addition, allocation of
\mathsf{NeiSub}
can be derived from a cycle of a cycle type
\mathsf{CycTyp}=(C,\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C},\mathsf{RobTyp})\in\mathsf{CycTypS}
, if
\mathsf{NeiSub}=\{v,v^{\prime}\}
,
\{\{u^{*},v\},\{u^{*},v^{\prime}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
and
\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C}(\{\{u^{*},v\},\{u^{*},v^{\prime}\}\})=\mathsf{VerTyp}
. Observe that a robot or a cycle might allocate
\mathsf{NeiSub}
to a vertex type
\mathsf{VerTyp}
multiple times. In the following definition, we derive for every
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
and
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
the set of total allocations of
\mathsf{NeiSub}
to
\mathsf{VerTyp}
from both robots and cycles.

Definition 59

(
Deriving Subsets from an ILP Solution).

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Let
x_{z}
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, be values that satisfy the inequalities of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
. Then, for every
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
, and every
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
,
\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}(\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\},\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub})=\{(\mathsf{CycTyp},i,t)

|~1\leq j\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|,\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},j),1\leq i\leq x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}},1\leq t\leq j\}\cup\{(i,t)~|~1\leq j\leq 2^{|\mathsf{VC}|}+|\mathsf{VC}|^{2},1\leq i\leq k,\mathsf{ILPDerRobTyp}(i)\in\mathsf{RobTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},j),1\leq t\leq j\}
.

Whenever
\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\}
is clear from context, we refer to
\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}(\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\},\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub})
as

\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub})
.

Now, we allocate the subsets we derived in Definition
59
to vertices. For every
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
and
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
, we arbitrary allocate each
\mathsf{Sub}\in\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}

(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub})
to a vertex in
u^{*}
, while ensuring that each vertex of a vertex type
\mathsf{VerTyp}
gets at least one item allocated.

Definition 60

(
Deriving Vertex Allocation of
\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}
from an ILP Solution).

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Let
x_{z}
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, be values that satisfy the inequalities of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
. For every
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
and every
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
, let
\mathsf{SubAlloc}_{\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub}}:\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub})\rightarrow u^{*}
be a function such that:

•

For every
u\in u^{*}
such that
\mathsf{ILPDerVerTyp}(u)=\mathsf{VerTyp}
, there exists

\mathsf{Sub}\in\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}

(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub})
such that
\mathsf{SubAlloc}_{\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub}}(\mathsf{Sub})=u
.

Then, for every
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
,
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
and
\mathsf{Sub}\in\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}

(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub})
,

\mathsf{ILPDerSubAlloc}(\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\},\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{Sub})=

\mathsf{SubAlloc}_{\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub}}(\mathsf{Sub})
.

Whenever
\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\}
is clear from context, we refer to
\mathsf{ILPDerSubAlloc}(\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\},\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{Sub})
as

\mathsf{ILPDerSubAlloc}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{Sub})
.

Recall that, by Equation
3
, for every
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
, and every
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
,

\displaystyle{\sum_{j=1}^{2{|\mathsf{VC}|}}\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},j)}}j\cdot x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}+}

\displaystyle{\sum_{j=1}^{2^{|\mathsf{VC}|}+|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}}}

\displaystyle{\sum_{{\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},j)}}j\cdot x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}\geq x_{\mathsf{VerTyp}}}
.

Observe that the left member of the equation equals to the number of allocations of
\mathsf{NeiSub}
we have for vertices of vertex type
\mathsf{VerTyp}
. That is,

\displaystyle{|\mathsf{ILPDerSubAlloc}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{Sub})|=\sum_{j=1}^{2{|\mathsf{VC}|}}\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},j)}}j\cdot x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}+}

\displaystyle{\sum_{j=1}^{2^{|\mathsf{VC}|}+|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}}\sum_{{\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},j)}}j\cdot x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}}
. So, since
x_{z}
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, satisfy Equations
3
, we have enough subsets to allocate. In addition,
u^{*}\neq\emptyset
. So, there exists a function
\mathsf{SubAlloc}_{\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub}}
as defined in Definition
60
. Therefore,
\mathsf{ILPDerSubAlloc}
is well defined.

Now, we look on the vertex allocation of
\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}
, defined in Definition
60
, from the perspective of the robots. Let
1\leq i\leq k
, and let
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})

\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
such that
\mathsf{ILPDerRobTyp}(i)=\mathsf{RobTyp}
. In addition,
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in 2^{\mathsf{N}_{G^{*}}(u^{*})\times\mathsf{2}}
,
\mathsf{VerTyp}\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
and
1\leq r\leq 2^{|\mathsf{VC}|}+|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}
such that
\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},r)
. Notice that this implies
\mathsf{SetOfSub}(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp})=\{(u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub})\in\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})

|~\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})}

((u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub}))=\mathsf{VerTyp}\}
has exactly
r
elements. So, there exists exactly
r
allocations of
\mathsf{NeiSub}
associated with the
i
-th robot in
\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub})
. In the following definition, we “execute” the allocations associated with the
i
-th robot, that is, we replace each
u_{j}^{*}\in V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}))
by a vertex
u\in u^{*}
, derived by
\mathsf{ILPDerSubAlloc}
. Observe that the allocations of the
i
-th robot in
\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub})
are labeled
1
to
r
. That is,
(i,1),\ldots,(i,r)\in\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub})
. So, first we arbitrary label each element in
\mathsf{SetOfSub}(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp})
by some unique
t\in[r]
, and then we replace the vertex by
\mathsf{ILPDerSubAlloc}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},(i,t))
.

Recall that a permutation of a multiset
A
is a bijection
\mathsf{Permut}_{A}:A\rightarrow[A]
.

Definition 61

(
Deriving a Transformation of
\mathsf{CC}
from an ILP Solution).

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Let
x_{z}
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, be values that satisfy the inequalities of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
. Let
1\leq i\leq k
, and let
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
such that
\mathsf{ILPDerRobTyp}(i)=\mathsf{RobTyp}
. For every
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in 2^{\mathsf{N}_{G^{*}}(u^{*})\times\mathsf{2}}
,
\mathsf{VerTyp}\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
and
1\leq r\leq 2^{|\mathsf{VC}|}+|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}
such that
\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},r)
, let
\mathsf{SetOfSub}(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp})=\{(u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub})\in\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})~|~\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})}((u^{*}_{j},

\mathsf{NeiSub}))=\mathsf{VerTyp}\}
and let
\mathsf{Permut}_{u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp}}:\mathsf{SetOfSub}(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp})\rightarrow[r]
be a permutation. Now, let
G^{\prime}
be the multigraph obtained from
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})
by replacing each
u^{*}_{j}
by
\mathsf{ILPDerSubAlloc}

(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},(i,\mathsf{Permut}_{u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp}}(u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub}))
, where
(u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub})\in\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}))
and
\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})}(u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub})=\mathsf{VerTyp}
. Then,
\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(\{x_{z}~|

z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\},i)=E(G^{\prime})
.

Whenever
\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\}
is clear from context, we refer to
\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\},i)
as
\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)
.

Let
1\leq i\leq k
, and let
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
such that
\mathsf{ILPDerRobTyp}(i)=\mathsf{RobTyp}
. In the next lemma, we show that
\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)
has the properties which will be useful later: (i)
\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)
is a multiset with elements from
E(G)
, (ii)
|\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)|=|\mathsf{CC}|
, (iii) vertices from
\mathsf{VC}
are not replaced, and (iv) the properties of
\mathsf{CC}
given by Condition
2
of Definition
43
are maintained also in
\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)
.

Lemma 62
.

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Let
x_{z}
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, be values that satisfy the inequalities of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
. Let
1\leq i\leq k
, and let
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
such that
\mathsf{ILPDerRobTyp}(i)=\mathsf{RobTyp}
. Then, the following conditions hold:

1.

\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)
is a multiset with elements from
E(G)
.

2.

|\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)|=|\mathsf{CC}|
.

3.

V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}))\cap\mathsf{VC}=V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{ILPToRobExp}(i))\cap\mathsf{VC}
.

4.

v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)))
,
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i))
is connected and every vertex in
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i))
has even degree in it.

Proof.

We prove that
\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)
is a multiset with elements from
E(G)
. By Condition
1
of Definition
43
,
\mathsf{CC}\subseteq E(\overline{G})
. Therefore, it is enough to show that each
u_{j}^{*}\in V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}))
is replaced by some
u\in u^{*}
. Let
u_{j}^{*}\in V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}))
. By Definition
40
,
(u^{*}_{i},\widehat{\mathsf{N}}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})}(u^{*}_{i}))\in\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}))
. Since
\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})}
is a vertex allocation of
\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}))
, by Definition
41
, there exists
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
such that
\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})}((u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub}))=\mathsf{VerTyp}
, where
\mathsf{NeiSub}=\widehat{\mathsf{N}}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})}(u^{*}_{i})\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
. Thus, there exists
1\leq r\leq 2^{|\mathsf{VC}|}+|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}
such that
\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},r)
.

Now, let
\mathsf{SetOfSub}(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp})=\{(u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub})\in\mathsf{NeiOfInd}(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})~|

\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})}

((u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub}))=\mathsf{VerTyp}\}
and let
\mathsf{Permut}_{u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp}}:\mathsf{SetOfSub}(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},

\mathsf{VerTyp})\rightarrow[r]
be the permutation defined in Definition
61
. Let
t\in[r]
be such that
\mathsf{Permut}_{u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp}}(u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub})=t
. Since
\mathsf{RobTyp}\in\mathsf{RobTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},r)
, by Definition
59
,
(i,1),\ldots,(i,r)\in\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}

(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub})
. By Definition
61
,
u_{j}^{*}
is replaced by
\mathsf{ILPDerSubAlloc}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},(i,t))
, and by Definition
60
,
\mathsf{ILPDerSubAlloc}(

\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},(i,t))\in u^{*}
. Therefore, Condition
1
holds.

Observe that
|\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)|=|\mathsf{CC}|
, so Condition
2
holds. In addition, it is easy to see, by Definition
61
, that only vertices from
V(G)\setminus\mathsf{VC}
are replaced, so Condition
3
holds.

By Condition
2
of Definition
43
,
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})
. Recall that we assume
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in\mathsf{VC}
, so
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)))
. In addition, by Condition
2
of Definition
43
,
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})
is connected. Thus, observe that
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i))
is also connected. Now, we show that every vertex in
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i))
has even degree in it. By Condition
2
of Definition
43
, every vertex in
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})
has even degree in it. Let
u\in V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)))
. If
u\in\mathsf{VC}
, observe that its degree in

\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i))
is equal to its degree in
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})
, so it is even. Otherwise
u\in u^{*}\in\mathsf{EQ}
, and its degree in
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i))
equals to the sum of degrees of the vertices
u^{*}_{j}
in
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})
, which are replaced by
u
, by Definition
61
. The degree of each such
u^{*}_{j}
is even in
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})
, so the degree of
u
in
\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i))
is also even. Therefore, Condition
4
holds.
∎

Now, we look at the vertex allocation of
\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}
, defined in Definition
60
, from the perspective of the cycles. In the next definition we execute a processes similar to Definition
61
. Let
\mathsf{CycTyp}=(C,\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C},\mathsf{RobTyp})\in\mathsf{CycTypS}
and let
1\leq i\leq x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}
. In addition, let
\mathsf{NeiSub}=\{v,v^{\prime}\}\in 2^{\mathsf{N}_{G^{*}}(u^{*})\times\mathsf{2}}
,
\mathsf{VerTyp}\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
and let
1\leq r\leq 2{|\mathsf{VC}|}
such that
\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},r)
. Notice that this implies
\mathsf{SetOfSub}(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp})=\{\{\{u^{*},v\},\{u^{*},v^{\prime}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)~|~\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C}=\mathsf{VerTyp}\}
has exactly
r
elements. So, there exists exactly
r
allocations of
\mathsf{NeiSub}
associated with the
i
-th cycle of
\mathsf{CycTyp}
in
\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub})
. In the following definition, we “execute” the allocations associated with the
i
-th cycle of
\mathsf{CycTyp}
, that is, we replace each
u^{*}\in V(C)
by a vertex
u\in u^{*}
, derived by
\mathsf{ILPDerSubAlloc}
. Observe that the allocations of the
i
-th cycle of
\mathsf{CycTyp}
in
\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub})
are labeled
1
to
r
. That is,
(\mathsf{CycTyp},i,1),\ldots,(\mathsf{CycTyp},i,r)\in\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub})
. So, first we arbitrary label each element in
\mathsf{SetOfSub}(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp})
by some unique
t\in[r]
, and then we replace the vertex by
\mathsf{ILPDerSubAlloc}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},(\mathsf{CycTyp},i,t))
.

Definition 63

(
Deriving a Transformation of a Cycle from an ILP Solution).

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Let
x_{z}
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, be values that satisfy the inequalities of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
. Let
\mathsf{CycTyp}=(C,\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C},\mathsf{RobTyp})\in\mathsf{CycTypS}
and let
1\leq i\leq x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}
. For every
\mathsf{NeiSub}=\{v,v^{\prime}\}\in 2^{\mathsf{N}_{G^{*}}(u^{*})\times\mathsf{2}}
,
\mathsf{VerTyp}\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
and
1\leq r\leq 2{|\mathsf{VC}|}
such that
\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},r)
, let
\mathsf{SetOfSub}(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp})=\{\{\{u^{*},v\},\{u^{*},v^{\prime}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)~|~\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C}=\mathsf{VerTyp}\}
and let
\mathsf{Permut}_{u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp}}:\mathsf{SetOfSub}(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp})\rightarrow[r]
be a permutation. Then,
\mathsf{ILPDerCycTransf}(\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\},\mathsf{CycTyp},i)
is the cycle obtained from
C
by replacing each
u^{*}\in V(C)
by
\mathsf{ILPDerSubAlloc}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\{v,v^{\prime}\},(\mathsf{CycTyp},i,

\mathsf{Permut}_{u^{*},\{v,v^{\prime}\},\mathsf{VerTyp}}(u^{*},\{\{u^{*},v\},\{u^{*},

v^{\prime}\}\})
, where
v
and
v^{\prime}
are the two vertices that appear before and after
u^{*}
in
C
, respectively.

Whenever
\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\}
is clear from context, we refer to
\mathsf{ILPDerCycTransf}(\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\},\mathsf{CycTyp},i)
as
\mathsf{ILPDerCycTransf}(

\mathsf{CycTyp},i)
.

Let
\mathsf{CycTyp}=(C,\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C},\mathsf{RobTyp})\in\mathsf{CycTypS}
and let
1\leq i\leq x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}
. In the next lemma, we show that
\mathsf{ILPDerCycTransf}(\mathsf{CycTyp},i)
has some properties that will be useful later.

Lemma 64
.

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Let
x_{z}
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, be values that satisfy the inequalities of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
. Let
\mathsf{CycTyp}=(C,\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C},\mathsf{RobTyp})\in\mathsf{CycTypS}
and let
1\leq i\leq x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}
. Then, the following conditions hold:

1.

\mathsf{ILPDerCycTransf}(\mathsf{CycTyp},i)
is a cycle in
G
.

2.

|C|=|\mathsf{ILPDerCycTransf}(\mathsf{CycTyp},i)|
.

3.

V(C)\cap\mathsf{VC}=V(\mathsf{ILPDerCycTransf}(\mathsf{CycTyp},i))\cap\mathsf{VC}
.

Proof.

We prove that
\mathsf{ILPDerCycTransf}(\mathsf{CycTyp},i)
is a cycle in
G
. By Definition
48
,
C\in\mathsf{Cyc}_{G^{*}}
, so it is enough to show that every vertex
u^{*}\in V(C)
is replaced by some
u\in u^{*}
. Let
u^{*}\in V(C)
. Let
v
and
v^{\prime}
be the two vertices that appear before and after
u^{*}
in
C
, respectively, and let
\mathsf{NeiSub}=\{v,v^{\prime}\}
.
By Definition
36
,
\{\{u^{*},v\},\{u^{*},v^{\prime}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
. Since
\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C}
is a vertex allocation of
\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)
, by Definition
45
, there exists
\mathsf{VerTyp}=(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSubsets})\in\mathsf{VerTypS}
such that
\mathsf{NeiSub}\in\mathsf{NeiSubsets}
and
\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C}(\{\{u^{*},v\},\{u^{*},v^{\prime}\}\})=\mathsf{VerTyp}
. Thus, there exists
1\leq r\leq|\mathsf{VC}|^{2}
such that
\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},r)
.

Now, let
\mathsf{SetOfSub}(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp})=\{\{\{u^{*},v\},\{u^{*},v^{\prime}\}\}\in\mathsf{EdgePairs}(C)~|~\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C}

=\mathsf{VerTyp}\}
and let
\mathsf{Permut}_{u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp}}:\mathsf{SetOfSub}(u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp})\rightarrow[r]
be the permutation defined in Definition
63
. Let
t\in[r]
be such that
\mathsf{Permut}_{u^{*},\mathsf{NeiSub},\mathsf{VerTyp}}(u^{*}_{j},\mathsf{NeiSub})=t
.
Since
\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},r)
, by Definition
59
,
(\mathsf{CycTyp},i,1),\ldots,(\mathsf{CycTyp},i,

r)\in\mathsf{ILPDerSubToAlloc}

(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub})
. By Definition
63
,
u_{j}^{*}
is replaced by

\mathsf{ILPDerSubAlloc}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},(\mathsf{CycTyp},i,t))
, and by Definition
60
,

\mathsf{ILPDerSubAlloc}(\mathsf{VerTyp},\mathsf{NeiSub},(\mathsf{CycTyp},i,t))\in u^{*}
. Therefore, Condition
1
holds.

Observe that
|C|=|\mathsf{ILPDerCycTransf}(\mathsf{CycTyp},i)|
, so Condition
2
holds. In addition, it is easy to see, by Definition
63
, that only vertices from
V(G)\setminus\mathsf{VC}
are replaced, so Condition
3
holds.
∎

Next, we allocate the cycles to the robots. We allocate cycles of type
\mathsf{CycTyp}=(C,\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C},\mathsf{RobTyp})\in\mathsf{CycTypS}
to robots of type
\mathsf{RobTyp}
, while preserving the budget limitation of the robots:
Each robot of type
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
gets exactly
N_{j}
cycles of length
j
, for every
2\leq j\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|
,
j\neq 4
, where
\mathsf{NumOfCyc}=(N_{2},N_{3},N_{5},N_{6},\ldots,N_{2|\mathsf{VC}|})
. Then, the cycles of type
\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{RobTyp},4)
are allocated to robots of type
\mathsf{RobTyp}
“equally”. That is, the number of cycles of length
4
allocated to a robot
i
of type
\mathsf{RobTyp}
is larger by at most
1
than the number of cycles of length
4
allocated to other robot
i^{\prime}
of type
\mathsf{RobTyp}
.

Definition 65

(
Deriving Robot Allocation of Cycles from an ILP Solution).

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Let
x_{z}
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, be values that satisfy the inequalities of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
. For every
\mathsf{CycTyp}=(C,\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C},\mathsf{RobTyp})\in\mathsf{CycTypS}
, let
\mathsf{CycAlloc}_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}:[x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}]\rightarrow[k]
be a function such that the following conditions hold:

1.

For every
i\in[x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}],\mathsf{ILPDerRobTyp}(\mathsf{CycAlloc}_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}(i))=\mathsf{RobTyp}
.

2.

For every
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
,
2\leq j\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|
,
j\neq 4
, and
i\in[k]
such that
\mathsf{ILPDerRobTyp}(i)=\mathsf{RobTyp}
, it holds that

\displaystyle{\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{RobTyp},j)}}|\{t\in[x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}]~|~\mathsf{CycTypFun}_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}(t)=i\}|=N_{j}}
,

where
\mathsf{NumOfCyc}=(N_{2},N_{3},N_{5},N_{6},\ldots,N_{2|\mathsf{VC}|})
.

3.

For every
i,i^{\prime}\in[k]
such that
\mathsf{ILPDerRobTyp}(i)=\mathsf{ILPDerRobTyp}(i^{\prime})=\mathsf{RobTyp}
, it holds that

\displaystyle{|\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{RobTyp},4)}}}

\displaystyle{|\{t\in[x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}]~|~\mathsf{CycAlloc}_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}(t)=i\}|-}

\displaystyle{\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{RobTyp},4)}}|\{t\in[x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}]~|~\mathsf{CycAlloc}_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}(t)=i^{\prime}\}||\leq 1}
.

Then, for every
\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}
and
i\in[x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}]
,

\mathsf{ILPDerCycAlloc}(\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\},\mathsf{CycTyp},i)=\mathsf{CycAlloc}_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}(i)
.

Whenever
\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\}
is clear from context, we refer to
\mathsf{ILPDerCycAlloc}(\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\},\mathsf{CycTyp},i)
as
\mathsf{ILPDerCycAlloc}(

\mathsf{CycTyp},i)
.

Observe that we assume
x_{z}
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, satisfy Equations
5
and
6
. So, for every
\mathsf{CycTyp}=(C,\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C},\mathsf{RobTyp})\in\mathsf{CycTypS}
such that
x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}\geq 1
, there exists
j\in[k]
such that
\mathsf{ILPDerRobTyp}(j)=\mathsf{RobTyp}
. In addition, by Equations
5
, for every
\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})\in\mathsf{RobTypS}
and for every
2\leq j\leq 2|\mathsf{VC}|
,
j\neq 4
,
\displaystyle{\sum_{{\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}(\mathsf{RobTyp},j)}}x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}=N_{j}\cdot x_{\mathsf{RobTyp}}}
,

where
\mathsf{NumOfCyc}=(N_{2},N_{3},N_{5},N_{6},\ldots,N_{2|\mathsf{VC}|})
.
Thus, there exist some functions

\mathsf{CycAlloc}_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}:[x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}]\rightarrow[k]
, for every
\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}
, as defined in Definition
65
. Therefore,
\mathsf{ILPDerCycAlloc}
is well defined.

Lastly, we present the function
\mathsf{ILPToRobExp}
. This function gets
i\in[k]
as input, and returns the multiset of edges, derived by the functions defined in this section, for the
i
-th robot. In particular, each robot gets the “transformed”
\mathsf{CC}
, where
\mathsf{ILPDerRobTyp}(i)=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})
, and the “transformed” cycles, allocated to the
i
-th robot by Definition
65
.

Definition 66

(
\mathsf{ILPToRobExp}
).

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Let
x_{z}
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, be values that satisfy the inequalities of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
.
Then, for every
1\leq i\leq k
,
\mathsf{ILPToRobExp}(\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\},i)=\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)\cup\{E(\mathsf{ILPDerCycTransf}(\mathsf{CycTyp},j))~|~\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS},j\in[x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}]
such that
\mathsf{ILPDerCycAlloc}(

\mathsf{CycTyp},j)=i\}
.

Whenever
\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\}
is clear from context, we refer to
\mathsf{ILPToRobExp}(\{x_{z}~|~z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}\},i)
as
\mathsf{ILPToRobExp}(i)
.

Towards the proof of Lemma
56
, we prove that the multisets defined in Definition
66
satisfy the conditions of Lemma
15
:

Lemma 67
.

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Let
x_{z}
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, be values that satisfy the inequalities of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
.
Then,
\mathsf{ILPToRobExp}(1),\ldots,\mathsf{ILPToRobExp}(k)
are multisets which satisfy the conditions of Lemma
15
.

For the sake of readability, we split Lemma
67
and its proof into an observation and three lemmas. In Observation
68
we prove that
\mathsf{ILPToRobExp}(i)
is a multiset with elements from
E(G)
. In Lemmas
69
we prove that Conditions
1
and
2
of Lemma
15
hold. In Lemmas
70
and
71
we prove that Conditions
3
and
4
of Lemma
15
hold, respectively.

Observation 68
.

Let
1\leq i\leq k
. Then,
\mathsf{ILPToRobExp}(i)
is a multiset with elements from
E(G)
.

Proof.

By Condition
1
of Definition
62
,
\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)
is a multiset with elements from
E(G)
.
In addition, for every
\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS}
and
j\in[x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}]
, by Condition
1
of Definition
64
,
\mathsf{ILPDerCycTransf}(\mathsf{CycTyp},j)
is a cycle in
G
, so, indeed,
E(\mathsf{ILPDerCycTransf}(\mathsf{CycTyp},

j))
is a multiset with elements from
E(G)
.
∎

Lemma 69
.

Let
G
be a connected graph, let
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(G)
, let
k,B\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathsf{VC}
be a vertex cover of
G
. Let
x_{z}
, for every
z\in\mathsf{VerTypS}\cup\mathsf{RobTypS}\cup\mathsf{CycTypS}
, be values that satisfy the inequalities of
\mathsf{Reduction}(G,v_{\mathsf{init}},k,B)
.
Then,
\mathsf{ILPToRobExp}(1),\ldots,\mathsf{ILPToRobExp}(k)
are multisets that satisfy Conditions
1
and
2
of Lemma
15
.

Proof.

Let
1\leq i\leq k
. By Condition
4
of Lemma
62
,
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)))
, so
v_{\mathsf{init}}\in V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{ILPToRobExp}(i))
. Therefore, Condition
1
of Lemma
15
is satisfied.

Let
\mathsf{ILPDerRobTyp}(i)=\mathsf{RobTyp}=(\mathsf{CC},\mathsf{Alloc}_{\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC})},\mathsf{NumOfCyc})
.
We prove that
\mathsf{Graph}(

\mathsf{ILPToRobExp}(i))
is connected. By Condition
4
of Lemma
62
,
\mathsf{Graph}

(\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i))
is connected; so, it is enough to show that, for every
v\in V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{ILPToRobExp}(i)))\setminus V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)))
, there exists a path from
v
to some

u\in V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)))
. Since
\mathsf{ILPToRobExp}(i)=\mathsf{ILPDerCCTransf}(i)\cup

\{E(\mathsf{ILPDerCycTransf}(\mathsf{CycTyp},j))~|~\mathsf{CycTyp}\in\mathsf{CycTypS},j\in[x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}]
such that

\mathsf{ILPDerCycAlloc}(\mathsf{CycTyp},j)=i\}
, there exists
\mathsf{CycTyp}=(C,\mathsf{PaAlloc}_{C},\mathsf{RobTyp})\in\mathsf{CycTypS},j\in[x_{\mathsf{CycTyp}}]
such that
\mathsf{ILPDerCycAlloc}(\mathsf{CycTyp},j)=i
and
v\in V(\mathsf{ILPDerCycTransf}(\mathsf{CycTyp},j))
. Now, since
\mathsf{ILPDerCycAlloc}(\mathsf{CycTyp},j)=i
, from Condition
1
of Definition
65
,

\mathsf{ILPToRobExp}(i)=\mathsf{RobTyp}
. So, from Definition
48
,
V(\mathsf{Graph}(\mathsf{CC}))\cap V(C)\cap\mathsf{VC}\neq\emptyset
.

Now, by Condition
3
of Lemma
64
,
